Title: Some Thoughts concerning the present Revival of Religion in New-England, and the way in which it ought to be acknowledged and promoted, humbly offered to the publick, in a treatise on that subject, in five parts
Creator: Jonathan Edwards (1703-1758)
Publisher: Boston: printed and sold by S. Kneeland and T. Green in Queen Street
Publication Date: 1742
Rights: Public Domain (published 1742, more than 95 years ago; public domain by date under the project's rolling rule; the item carries no rights field, so the date rule alone applies)
Source: Internet Archive item somethought00edwa (https://archive.org/details/somethought00edwa), the item's own somethought00edwa_djvu.txt full-text OCR derivative; contributing library per the item metadata: Princeton Theological Seminary Library; host metadata date: 1742. Fetched directly (curl/urllib, raw bytes) by the Library thread (Era VIII pass), 2026-10-23. sha256 of the raw fetched bytes: 6bd84621fb1956cc7bf414a15f2a7e5b39778f68898380f948a1d5bd3b16df5c.
Provenance note: Header prepended by the Library thread; nothing else altered, no OCR error corrected, nothing retyped. Princeton Theological Seminary scan of the first edition; clean OCR after garbled library marks, with the long s rendered as f.
Content note: Edwards's full-length defence and criticism of the awakening at its height, including the account of a person's experience (his wife Sarah's, unnamed) and his counsel on the errors of the revival's friends; the movement examining itself in 1742.
